# 3D building data for Northern and Eastern Europe (Germany, the Nordics, the Baltics, Poland to Serbia, Ukraine, Belarus, Russia, the Caucasus, Turkey)

Run date 2026-11-13. Read-only research (task #1326) for Scenic Roads. Method: four sub-agents (Sonnet, medium) by area (Germany; the Nordics and Baltics with the Overture sample; Poland, Czechia, Slovakia, Hungary, Romania, Bulgaria, Moldova, Serbia; Ukraine, Belarus, Russia, the Caucasus, Turkey). Each fetched pages and API answers with User-Agent `scenic-roads/0.1 (personal offline map)`. I merged their parts into one table per region (the Europe-W report's 12 columns), edited the rows, notes and lists by hand so that each says what is true now, checked that every table row has its header's cell count (0 mismatches), and wrote the headline from the finished tables. Global datasets (Microsoft, Google, GHSL, GBA, 3D-GloBFP, WSF3D, Esri, Cesium, Google 3D Tiles) as in the Americas and US reports; EUBUCCO and INSPIRE BU as in buildings-europe-w.md section 3 (not repeated). Western and Southern Europe is task #1325. Sections: 2-5 the region tables (2 Germany, 3 Nordics and Baltics, 4 Poland to Serbia, 5 Ukraine to Turkey); 6 notes; 7 nothing better found; 8 non-governmental; 9 the Overture sample; 10 overlaps; 11 Unverified; 12 accidental; 13 URLs. Sub-section numbers inside sections 6-8 and 10-12 follow the same four areas (1 Germany, 2 Nordics and Baltics, 3 Poland to Serbia, 4 Ukraine to Turkey).

## 1. Headline

- **Germany is where the measured LoD2 is, and it is open Land by Land, not federally.** BKG's LoD2-DE (58.6 M buildings by the per-Land counts in its documentation, April 2026), LoD1-DE and HU-DE footprints are restricted to federal and Länder authorities ("nur einem eingeschränkten Kreis Nutzungsberechtigter"); no federal building product is on BKG's open-data list. The Länder's own LoD2 is open with a licence read in 14 of 16: dl-de/zero-2.0 (Berlin, Hessen, NRW), dl-de/by-2-0 (Baden-Württemberg, Brandenburg, Hamburg, Rheinland-Pfalz, Saarland, Sachsen-Anhalt, Sachsen per GeoSN's terms page), CC BY 4.0 (Bayern, Bremen, Mecklenburg-Vorpommern, Niedersachsen); Thüringen CC BY 4.0 (the Land's CSW record; its tile service answered an outage page) and Schleswig-Holstein has no open LoD2 found (INSPIRE ALKIS footprints with storeys on 66 % of 2.52 M buildings, CC BY 4.0). Counts: Bayern 18,297 tiles (152.6 GB, about 9.8 M objects), NRW 35,022 tiles (219.6 GB; also an OGC API serving CityJSON, glTF and 3D Tiles by bbox), Niedersachsen 37,940 tiles (146.7 GB, public S3 bucket), Brandenburg 19,298 tiles (4.78 GB), Berlin 925 tiles. Heights are standard-roof LoD2, `measuredHeight` = highest roof point above ground (checked on a Bayern tile).
- **Where it adds most (overlap, one tile or box each, Overture release 2026-09-23.1):** Cologne/NRW 3,230 buildings, 96 % add a height (Overture height on 4.7 % of matched; LoD2 runs 1.21 times Overture's); Munich/Bayern 2,639, 87 % add a height, plus storeys on 63.6 % of Bayern LoD2 buildings (689 add storeys; ratio 1.48); Berlin Mitte 82 % (ratio 1.20; only 56 % of its 722 buildings match an Overture footprint). Brandenburg is the exception: Overture's heights already match the state LoD2 (Potsdam ratio 0.99, 82 % within 20 %; only 8 % add a height). Overture height share in German boxes: Cologne 4.4 %, Munich 10.2 %, Berlin 14.6 %, Hamburg 20.3 %, rural Waldeck-Frankenberg 42.6 %.  Niedersachsen (Hannover tile LoD2_32_548_5803: 598 buildings, 78.6 % matched, adds a height to 70.2 %, LoD2 1.12 times Overture, 56.2 % within 20 %). LoD2 attributes opened per Land: Niedersachsen and Mecklenburg-Vorpommern have measuredHeight, roofType and function but no storeys or year; Bremen (Bremerhaven tile of 5,081 buildings) has storeys on 73.8 %; Hessen and Saarland WFS samples have heights and no storeys. Sachsen licence read on GeoSN's terms page (dl-de/by-2-0, attribution "GeoSN"); Thüringen CC BY 4.0 "© GDI-Th" from the Land's CSW record. Schleswig-Holstein: no LoD2 found, the "opened in 2024" claim is neither confirmed nor refuted (its hosts do not connect); no city model found for Frankfurt, Düsseldorf, Nuremberg or Munich; Cologne's 2010 model was not read.
- **Textured and LoD3 beyond the buildings layer:** Hamburg LoD3.0-HH (about 531,000 models) and a 13.2 GB 3D Tiles mesh (dl-de/by-2-0); Bayern DOM-Mesh (8.3 TB, CC BY 4.0); NRW mesh (108 SLPK, 11.36 TB, dl-de/zero-2.0); Brandenburg 3D Tiles mesh (dl-de/by-2-0); Berlin's mesh is "other-closed" with unreadable terms; Stuttgart's city model is explicitly not open. City models: Hannover (LoD2, CC BY), Leipzig, Freiburg, Aachen, Dresden (open; mostly duplicates the Land's); Munich, Frankfurt, Düsseldorf and Nuremberg have no separate open model. Storeys: Bayern LoD2, Brandenburg and Schleswig-Holstein INSPIRE ALKIS; NRW's open ALKIS has none.
- **The Nordics are mostly not open for heights.** Estonia is the clear exception: the Land Board's LoD2 (856,360 buildings, CityGML/GDB/OBJ, free, commercial use and redistribution allowed; `measuredHeight` = roof maximum minus building minimum, verified on all 49,816 Tallinn buildings) adds a height to 872 of 5,565 Tallinn buildings (15.7 %); Overture's Tallinn already has heights on 75.5 % (OSM edits), and the two agree to a median 0.58 m. Riga's LoD1 (116,659 objects, CC-BY-4.0) adds a height to 5,074 of 6,723 buildings and runs 1.72 m above Overture's. Helsinki's CityGML (CC BY 4.0, city-wide pages 403/404 from here; the 2019 Kalasatama file measured) adds a height to 1,731 of 2,658. Denmark: no open height anywhere (INSPIRE Bygninger, 6.0 M footprints, height and floor fields empty; "Danmark i 3D" unfunded); the BBR INSPIRE points (5.65 M) carry floors, agree with Overture's num_floors on 76 % of pairs and add storeys to 5,048 Copenhagen buildings with neither. Norway: FKB-Bygning (2.5D) is restricted to Norge digitalt parties; only the Matrikkelen points (CC BY 4.0) and Trondheim's 2014 model (NLOD, 3ds Max) are open. Sweden: Lantmäteriet's footprints (1.3 GB, CC BY 4.0) have no height or floors and need credentials. Finland's NLS LoD2 covers a few example areas only; the topographic database gives a storey class. Latvia's (1,450,651) and Lithuania's (2.16 M) national footprints have no height or floors. Iceland, Faroes, Greenland, Svalbard and Åland: nothing usable.  Stockholm's SBK 3D-byggnader LOD1 is open and anonymous (shapefiles, 77,769 buildings, 17.8 MB; BYGG_H = median roof height above ground, checked as TAK_Z minus MARK_Z): in the central box it adds a height to 6,679 of 6,875 buildings (Overture has one on 196 matched, from OSM; the source is 1.6 m lower on those). Umeå's LOD1 (CC0, 22 GeoJSON tiles, `By_höjd` mean roof height) measured on one tile (Holmsund, 25,037 buildings; Overture has no height there). Norway's open Bygningspunkt WFS (4,418,904 points) has building type and status but no storeys or height. Helsinki: the Energy and Climate Atlas table (77,267 buildings, storeys on 46,917) is open but has no geometry and the city-wide CityGML stayed unreachable. Not reached: Gothenburg, Malmö, Uppsala, NLS storey counts, Iceland's register, Lithuanian and Latvian city models.
- **Central and south-east Europe: three real finds.** Slovakia's ÚGKK INSPIRE Buildings (3,611,432 polygons, 93.2 % with `heightAboveGround` = top of construction minus lowest ground point, CC BY 4.0, 418 MB zip) adds a height to 83 % of Bratislava's buildings; Overture's heights there run about 35 % lower (median ratio 1.53, 19 % within 20 %). Brno's "Výška budov" (70,512 buildings, DSM minus terrain, CC BY 4.0) adds a height to 99.5 % of its central box. Czechia's RÚIAN (4,205,805 polygons with `pocetpodlazi`, above plus below ground storeys, CC BY 4.0) and Prague's "Podlažnosti" (178,775 polygons, CC BY) add storeys to only 5.2 % (Brno) and 18.5 % (Prague) because Overture already has OSM floors there; Prague's LoD2-class model (CC BY) has no download link found. Bulgaria's cadastre INSPIRE service (3,284,195 buildings, every one with `numberoffloorsaboveground`) adds storeys to 77.7 % of Sofia's buildings, but no licence text was read. Czechia's national LoD2 (ZMVP) is not expected before 2030; Ostrava has LoD2.3 roofs as elevations above sea level only. Hungary, Romania, Moldova and Serbia: nothing usable (Hungary a 2014 one-area sample; Romania's ANCPI refuses connections; Serbia's RGZ is an address register).  Prague's model has no download link found (scene-layer metadata opened); Bulgaria's metadata gives a use limitation "Tariff № 14" with the fee text not found; Poznań and Wrocław are viewers only; Bratislava's 2021 web app is CC-BY-NC-ND (unusable); Moldova's CKAN has 19 cadastre datasets, none with geometry.
- **Poland, measured from GUGiK's own files** (Polish hosts still refuse connections from here and from the mini; the Internet Archive's CDX index and `id_` captures held the downloads, intermittently): LoD2 for Warsaw (138,830 buildings, laser 2012), Kraków (82,676, laser 2017) and radomski (90,677, laser 2011-14), and Warsaw LoD1 (145,508, laser 2018; this capture has no `measuredHeight`), plus the licence and description PDFs. LoD2 covers 236 of 380 counties (Warsaw, Kraków, Radom yes; Gdańsk, Łódź, Poznań, Wrocław LoD1 only). Licence: public-sector information under the 2016 re-use Act, no fees; name the Główny Geodeta Kraju and www.geoportal.gov.pl, give the retrieval date, state any processing and say the models are not a register; CC0 1.0 for what the Act leaves unregulated (attribution-only in effect; ODbL mixing not decided, keep as a separate layer). Overlap (centroid-in-footprint, one 0.05 degree box each): the models add a height to 95 % of Warsaw-centre buildings (Overture heights 5.5 %), 76 % in Kraków's centre (22.1 %, mostly Microsoft) and 99-100 % in two Radom-county boxes. LoD2 ridge matches OSM heights in Warsaw (ratio 1.02, 74 % within 20 %; eave 0.80, mean roof 0.93: the height meant shifts comparisons by 20-30 %); in Kraków Overture's Microsoft heights run low (ridge ratio 1.37). Overture already has floors on 83.5 % of Warsaw-centre buildings (OSM's BDOT10k imports), 48 % in Kraków, 38.5 % in a Radom box, so BDOT10k storeys add little there and the heights are the gain; the BDOT10k fill rate itself is unmeasured. Not reached: the current 2019-2024 LoD1 layers, BDOT10k files, and the cities' own models (only Poznań and Wrocław viewers; Warsaw, Kraków, Gdańsk, Łódź nothing found).
- **East of the Bug, and Turkey: no open per-building height or storey source was reachable.** Overture heights are OSM tags only (0.2 to 3.7 %): Kyiv 0.8 %, Minsk 3.7 %, Moscow 1.8 %, St Petersburg 0.8 %, Tbilisi 0.2 %, Istanbul 0.6 %, Ankara 2.1 %. num_floors is on 54 to 77 % of buildings in Moscow, Minsk, Kyiv and St Petersburg but only 16 % in Tbilisi and 2.1 % (Istanbul) and 6.4 % (Ankara) in Turkey, so Turkey has the biggest storey gap and no open national source (TKGM, the ministry's 3D city models and Istanbul's IBB portal did not connect or hold no data; Izmir's Bina Kimlik Belgesi CSV has no coordinates). Moscow's open address register has no storeys or heights; Ukraine's cadastre holds parcels, and Kyiv's and Lviv's lists have no geometry or storeys. 2GIS (floors field; clause 4.2 forbids storing results) and Yandex (30 days' cache) are unusable for an offline map. Most Russian, Belarusian, Georgian and Turkish hosts did not connect; Wayback, CKAN and ArcGIS Online were the routes that worked.  Izmir's Bina Kimlik Belgesi does carry storeys (`SERBEST_KAT_ADEDI`, filled on 24,687 of 24,688 Bayraklı rows and all 19,825 Bornova rows opened; truncated downloads; the meaning is inferred) but has no coordinates, so it cannot be joined to footprints without a geocoder (Overture's Izmir box has floors on 0.2 %). Reforma ZhKKh's open-data page (Wayback, 2022) lists per-house `floor_count_max`, `floor_count_min`, `built_year`, but files are e-mailed after registration, no licence text, nothing opened. Moscow's capital-construction set is a point set without storeys on the page read. Every host that failed here failed from the mini too.
- **Overture in the sample (section 9):** footprints and heights come only from OpenStreetMap, Microsoft ML Buildings and Esri Community Maps (Riga, Helsinki; footprints). Microsoft ML supplies most heights in Germany, the Nordics, Vilnius, Riga, Kraków and Bratislava; elsewhere (Prague, Budapest, Bucharest, Sofia and east, Istanbul, Warsaw) heights are OSM tags on 0.2-6 %. Buildings with neither height nor floors: Warsaw 15.7 %, Tallinn 19.8 %, Stockholm 19.0 %, Berlin 22.6 %, Cologne 49.5 %, Bucharest 82.4 %, Tbilisi 83.6 %, Ankara 93.5 %, Istanbul 97.5 %. The sample's two agents' boxes differ slightly for Prague (1.3 % vs 1.4 %) and Bratislava (10.8 % vs 11.9 %): the sample table is the reference.
- **ODbL mixing:** dl-de/zero-2.0, dl-de/by-2-0, CC BY 4.0, CC0, NLOD and Estonia's open-data licence are attribution-only and allow a personal offline cache; the OSM wiki rates CC BY 4.0 "likely incompatible" for contributing into OSM itself (a community page, not an OSMF ruling), so a separate attribution layer in a derived set is the cleaner reading (as in the Europe-W report). Not usable: BKG's federal products, FKB-Bygning, 2GIS, Yandex, Berlin's mesh (terms unread), Stuttgart. ETHOS.BUILDA (ODbL, 21.27 GB, all Germany with heights, a 2022-23 LoD2 snapshot) is ODbL-compatible but stale; GlobalBuildingAtlas LoD1 is CC BY-NC 4.0 (out).
- **What was measured from a real file and what is described from pages only.** Measured from opened files or samples: Bayern (Munich tile), NRW (Cologne), Berlin Mitte, Brandenburg (Potsdam, Lausitz), Niedersachsen (Hannover tile), Bremen (Bremerhaven), Mecklenburg-Vorpommern (Rostock tiles), Hessen, Saarland and Sachsen-Anhalt (WFS samples only); Estonia LOD2 (Tallinn), Riga LOD1, Helsinki Kalasatama 2019 (one district only), Stockholm LOD1, Umeå (one tile), Denmark's INSPIRE Bygninger and BBR; Slovakia, Brno, Prague "Podlažnosti", RÚIAN, Bulgaria's cadastre; Poland's GUGiK LoD2 (Warsaw, Kraków, radomski) and Warsaw LoD1 from Wayback copies of the files; Izmir's CSV. Described from pages or records only (nothing opened): BKG's federal products, Baden-Württemberg, Hamburg, Rheinland-Pfalz, Sachsen, Thüringen, Schleswig-Holstein, Berlin's mesh and the other Länder's LoD2 attributes not listed above; Helsinki city-wide and Espoo; Finland's NLS 3D; Norway's FKB; Stockholm LOD2, Helsingborg; Latvia's and Lithuania's national layers beyond counts; Prague's 3D model; Hungary, Romania, Moldova, Serbia; Reforma ZhKKh, Rosreestr, Moscow, St Petersburg, Ukraine, Belarus, the Caucasus and Turkey (all but Izmir).
- **Unverified and why:** Poland's current 2019-2024 LoD1 layers, BDOT10k files and city models (hosts refuse); Denmark's GeoDanmark/Datafordeler, Sweden's files and Dataforsyningen's OGC API (login or account, not created); Lithuanian city models (data.gov.lt bot block, not circumvented); Helsinki's city-wide pages and hri.fi/avoindata.fi (403); Sachsen's licence text, Thüringen's page (CAPTCHA), ten Länder's LoD2 storeys, Frankfurt/Düsseldorf/Nuremberg city models; Prague's download link; Bulgaria's licence; Romania and Hungary; all of Russia, Belarus, Caucasus and Turkey portals listed in section 11.

### 4.1 Poland

| Jurisdiction / product | Product, version | Publisher | Holds | Heights or storeys: how measured, what they mean | Completeness | Year | Format and access | Licence; cache?; ODbL-mixable? | Footprint/coverage-index URL | Vs Overture | Evidence URL(s) fetched |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Poland / 3D models of buildings, LoD2 ("Budynki LoD2") | CityGML 2.0, LoD2 solids with ground, wall and roof surfaces, one ZIP per county (powiat) named `<TERYT>_gml.zip`, tiles per 1:10,000 map sheet | Główny Urząd Geodezji i Kartografii (GUGiK), task "Budowa modeli 3D budynków" in the CAPAP project | LoD2 with roof shapes | Opened files: each building has `buildingId` (BDOT10k id), `przestNazw`, `wersjaId`, `zrodloDach` (ALS_I or ALS_II), `aktZrodla` (year of the laser data), `roofType` code, a `lod2Solid` and GroundSurface/WallSurface/RoofSurface polygons in EPSG:2180 with heights in PL-KRON86-NH; no height attribute, so height = roof z minus ground z. Measured on the files (roof vertices of each building minus mean ground z): ridge (highest roof point) median 17.2 m in Warsaw, 14.0 m Kraków, 6.1 m Radom county; eave (lowest roof point) 12.0, 8.5 and 3.3 m; mean of roof vertices 15.2, 11.6 and 4.7 m. Publisher: from footprints of BDOT10k BUBD_A, airborne laser data (4 or 12 points/m2) and a 1 m terrain model | Publisher: "for 236 counties covering the area of 10 voivodeship" (confirmed in the package list: 236 of 380 counties flagged LoD2, namely every county of voivodeships 04, 06, 12, 14, 16, 18, 20, 24, 26 and 28, so Warsaw, Kraków and Radom yes; Gdańsk, Łódź, Poznań, Wrocław no). Opened files: Warsaw (1465) 138,830 buildings, ALS_II of 2012; Kraków (1261) 82,676, ALS_II of 2017; radomski (1425) 90,677 (ALS_I 80,774 and ALS_II 9,903; laser years 2011, 2012, 2014) | Laser data 2011-2017; files in the 2018 state ("developed as of 2018"); Warsaw ZIP captured 2025-08-03 (112,554,466 bytes, 2.5 GB unzipped) | Download pattern from the R package `rgugik` source: `https://integracja.gugik.gov.pl/Budynki3D/pobierz.php?plik=powiaty/<TERYT>_gml.zip` (LoD2) and `.../powiaty/lod1/<TERYT>_gml.zip` (LoD1); also `https://opendata.geoportal.gov.pl/InneDane/Budynki3D/LOD2/1425_gml.zip`. These hosts still refuse connections here; the files above were read from Internet Archive captures (`web.archive.org/web/<stamp>id_/<url>`; the Archive answers intermittently, with 503 pages when offline). Also via the geoportal identify tool and the WMS `.../ModeleBudynkow3D` | Licence opened (GUGiK, "Warunki wykorzystania danych zawierających modele trójwymiarowe budynków", PDF of 2024-01-31): the models are public information, reusable under the 2016 Act on re-use of public sector information "without any fees and restrictions", provided the user (1) names the Główny Geodeta Kraju as holder and www.geoportal.gov.pl as source, (2) states the date of retrieval, (3) tells recipients about any processing and its extent, (4) tells recipients the models are not a public register and are indicative only; "to the extent not regulated" the Creative Commons CC0 1.0 terms apply (link in the PDF). Personal offline cache: allowed. | Turkey / Izmir Metropolitan Municipality open data: Bina Kimlik Belgesi (Bayrakli, Bornova) | CSV files: Bayrakli (prepared 13.11.2026; about 13.6 MB served) and Bornova (2024); CKAN record says target 903,803 buildings for all Izmir | Izmir Metropolitan Municipality | Per building: street address and number, block/parcel (ADA_PARSEL), use class, structural system, year built for earthquake basis, floor-count fields; no coordinates or geometry | storeys: SERBEST_KAT_ADEDI is filled on nearly every row (opened files: Bayrakli 24,687 of 24,688 rows read, values 1 to 10+, most 2 to 4; Bornova 19,825 of 19,825 rows read, same range); NORMAL_KAT_ADEDI and TOPLAM_KAT_ADEDI are filled on only 570 Bayrakli rows; the meaning of SERBEST_KAT_ADEDI ("free floors") is inferred as above-ground storeys, not stated on the pages read; no height | Bayrakli 24,688 rows (23,815 distinct building ids) of at least 32,558 (partial downloads cut by the server at 10.5 MB; the file may hold up to 32,558 rows) and Bornova 19,825 (download cut at 4.3 MB) | 2024-2026 | CSV, CKAN API | "CC BY 4.0 uluslararası lisansı ile İzmir Büyükşehir Belediyesi tarafından lisanslanmıştır" (portal licence page); cache yes; ODbL-mixable: as for CC BY | https://acikveri.bizizmir.com/api/3/action/package_show?id=bina-kimlik-belgesi | Not joinable to Overture footprints (no coordinates; a geocoder on street and number would be needed); Overture's Izmir box has floors on 0.2 % of 52,769 buildings, so these storeys would add a lot if geocoded; facts from opened files except the field's meaning | https://acikveri.bizizmir.com/api/3/action/package_show?id=bina-kimlik-belgesi ; https://openfiles.izmir.bel.tr/111661/docs/izbb-bina-kimlik-belgesi.csv ; https://acikveri.bizizmir.com/tr/license |
| Izmir / "İlçelere ait Bina Kat Sayıları" (district building-level floor counts) | XLS, 63 kB, modified 2026-04-03 | Izmir Metropolitan Municipality | Floor counts by district (the resource is named building-based, but the 63 kB size and "ilçelere ait" title indicate district aggregates; the XLS was not opened) | storeys, aggregated | not stated | 2026 | XLS | CC BY (CKAN licence id cc-by) | https://acikveri.bizizmir.com/api/3/action/package_show?id=ilcelere-ait-bina-kat-sayilari | Not usable for per-building heights | https://acikveri.bizizmir.com/api/3/action/package_show?id=ilcelere-ait-bina-kat-sayilari |

## 6. Notes per jurisdiction

### 6.1 Germany

**Federal.** BKG's LoD2-DE (produktstand 2026-04, 58.6 M buildings by the doc's per-Land counts) and LoD1-DE (frozen April 2024) and HU-DE are not open: the page states the product "steht nur einem eingeschränkten Kreis Nutzungsberechtigter zur Verfügung", free of money only to federal authorities (V GeoBund) and Länder authorities (V GeoLänder); HU-DE needs a licence agreement. The federal product is just the Länder's data passed once a year through the ZSHH (a unit at LDBV Bayern), so every usable building is in a Land's own open product. The doc lists Länder peculiarities: MV leaves `Name` empty; BuildingParts exist in BW, BE, BB, HE, NW, RP, SL, SN, ST, TH and not in BY, HB, HH, MV, NI, SH; addresses in BW, BY, HB, HH, NI, NW, RP, ST, SH, TH.

**What the height means.** The AdV standard defines Gebäudehöhe as roof height minus ground height, with the roof height the highest point (Bayern: measuredHeight = HoeheDach minus HoeheGrund, checked on a tile; NRW: highest minus lowest reference point). LoD1 heights are a mean of eave and ridge in NI, HH (derived from LoD2) and MV (median); BB's LoD1 uses the highest building point. A map that draws a ridge height from the state LoD2 will be taller than one drawn from Microsoft ML (ratio 1.2 to 1.6 in the overlap below).

**Baden-Württemberg.** LoD2 is "in der Regel Open Data" under dl-de/by-2-0, delivered from the Open GeoData portal (an Angular app; I did not find a machine-readable tile list). LGL itself says the LoD2 is not yet suitable for plastic 3D city models (roof-shape hit rate about 90 %). 6.48 M buildings. In ETHOS.BUILDA's 2022 build BW's footprints came from OSM, which suggests LoD2 was not downloadable in bulk then.

**Bayern.** Best-served Land: LoD2 for all of Bavaria as CC BY 4.0 with a Metalink of 18,297 2x2 km tiles (152.6 GB) refreshed weekly, direct HTTP tiles, no registration; storeysAboveGround on 63.6 % of buildings in the Munich tile I fetched; address and Traufe attribute present. Also the DOM-Mesh (textured, 8.3 TB, 102 flight lots) and Hausumringe. Heights are ridge-above-ground; per building the tile carries the lowest eave as well.

**Berlin.** LoD2 and LoD1 are dl-de/zero-2.0 ATOM downloads of 925 1x1 km tiles (UTM33), with building parts and no storeys or addresses in the tile. A 2025 multi-perspective-flight textured mesh (OBJ) exists, but its terms page was unreachable during portal maintenance and the record's licence is "other-closed".

**Brandenburg.** The most complete open set: LoD2 and LoD1 GML in 19,298 1x1 km tiles (4.78 GB) as dl-de/by-2-0, INSPIRE WFS (2,368,753 buildings), INSPIRE 2D ALKIS WFS with storeys on 56 % and heights on 36 % of a spread sample (2,485,114 buildings), Hausumringe semi-annually, and (Oct 2026) 3D Tiles meshes. Overture already carries almost equal heights here (see Overlap): the LoD2 adds little in height, but the 2D ALKIS WFS adds storeys.

**Bremen.** LoD2 and LoD1 for Bremen (403 MB zip) and Bremerhaven (78 MB, 36 tiles, 949 MB unpacked), CC BY 4.0, refreshed July 2026; Hausumringe CC BY 4.0. In Bremerhaven's largest tile (5,081 buildings, opened) measuredHeight, roofType, function and address are filled, storeysAboveGround is filled on 73.8 % (with a storeys-source code), and there is no construction year.

**Hamburg.** LoD2-DE yearly ZIPs (CityGML V1.0, 628 MB for 2026-04-28, dl-de/by-2-0), LoD1-DE frozen at 2023, LoD3.0-HH (about 531,000 models from the 2020 flight, textured 12.9 GB and untextured 2.3 GB) and a 13.2 GB 3D Tiles mesh, all dl-de/by-2-0. The only open citywide LoD3 and mesh among the big cities.

**Hessen.** LoD2/LoD1 from the HVBG Downloadcenter under dl-de/zero-2.0 (ISO metadata dated 2026-11-05); annual update; 5.0 M buildings. The INSPIRE 3D WFS (4,973,846 buildings) answers without login; a 100-feature sample has a height and a use on all 100 and no storeys. The Downloadcenter's native CityGML sits behind a registration I did not use, so its attributes are unverified.

**Mecklenburg-Vorpommern.** LoD2 via an INSPIRE-ATOM feed of 6,344 2x2 km CityGML tiles (CC BY 4.0, updated 2026-03-30; footprints "des Vorjahres"); LoD1 height is the median; selected photo-textured LoD3 "Landmarks" made by Geomatiker apprentices, CC BY 4.0 (KMZ). Three Rostock-area tiles opened (9,593 buildings): height, roof type and function are filled everywhere; no storeys, address or year. One corner tile of the ATOM list is empty (659 bytes).

**Niedersachsen.** LoD2 (complete 2019) and LoD1 under CC BY 4.0 through a public S3 bucket listing 37,940 1 km tiles (146.7 GB GML, 2024-25 stamps); the documented tile naming is LoD2_32_<x>_<y>_1_ni.gml. LoD1 height is the mean of ridge and eave; terrain height from a 5 m DGM. A Hannover tile (LoD2_32_548_5803_1_ni.gml, 693 buildings with 545 parts) opened: measuredHeight, roofType, function, address and the ridge and eave attributes are filled; storeys and year of construction are empty.

**Nordrhein-Westfalen.** The richest delivery: LoD2 GML (35,022 tiles, 219.6 GB), LoD1, an SLPK package, a 3D Tiles/CityJSON OGC API with bbox queries (11,605,500 buildings), I3S SceneServer and a textured mesh (108 SLPK files, 11.36 TB, 2022-2025), all dl-de/zero-2.0. Open ALKIS carries no storeys: the Bottrop NAS has 66,392 AX_Gebaeude and no storey element, the GPKG attribute `anzahlgs` is empty in 85,818 of 85,818 features, and the INSPIRE building has no floors or heights.

**Rheinland-Pfalz.** LoD2 and LoD1 under dl-de/by-2-0 via the Geoshop (open-data cart). **Saarland.** INSPIRE LoD2 WFS only (738,006 buildings), dl-de/by-2-0, no bulk file found; a 300-feature sample has a height on 97 % and no storeys, and the service is slow (over 100 s for 300 features). **Sachsen.** Free LoD1/LoD2 download in 2 km tiles including Bauwerke; footprints from ATKIS; dl-de/by-2-0 per GeoSN's own terms page (attribution "GeoSN", download free); tile count unknown. **Sachsen-Anhalt.** LoD2 as four ZIPs (4.54 GB), dl-de/by-2-0; the LoD2 WFS (30-feature sample) exposes only height, elevation and footprint, and the zips were not opened. **Schleswig-Holstein.** No open LoD2 found though LoD2-DE lists 2.33 M SH buildings: govdata (LoD2, 3D-Gebäudemodell), the Land's open-data CKAN and the GDI-SH CSW show none, and geodaten.schleswig-holstein.de does not connect. What is open is ALKIS Schleswig-Holstein without owner data (CC BY 4.0, storeys on 66 %, a vereinfacht ATOM feed, WMTS and an OGC API Features with buildings among the objects). A 2024 opening of LoD2 is neither confirmed nor refuted. **Thüringen.** LoD1/LoD2, CC BY 4.0 "© GDI-Th" (the Land's own CSW record); the geoportal-th ATOM and WFS entry points answered an outage page on 13 Nov, so a tile count and a sample tile were not obtained.

**Storeys ("Anzahl der oberirdischen Geschosse") in open data, measured.** Bayern LoD2: 63.6 % (Munich tile). Brandenburg INSPIRE 2D ALKIS: 56.0 % (2,000 spread sample). Schleswig-Holstein INSPIRE 2D ALKIS: 66.0 % (2,000). NRW: none (all NAS and GPKG features). Berlin and Brandenburg LoD2 tiles: none. Saarland and Sachsen-Anhalt INSPIRE 2D: none in the first 169 / 250 features. Niedersachsen and Mecklenburg-Vorpommern LoD2 tiles: none. Bremerhaven LoD2: 73.8 % of 5,081 buildings. Hessen and Saarland WFS samples: none. Sachsen-Anhalt's WFS does not expose a storey field. Not read: BW, HH, RP, SN and TH LoD2 (reasons in section 11). The federal doc calls storeys "nicht in jedem Land geführt und unvollständig".

**Counts that moved.** LoD2-DE counts 2025 to 2026: NI 6,870,978 to 7,324,012 (+453,034, "komplett" update); NW 11,606,005 to 11,870,213; ST 1,774,671 to 1,772,265 (down).

### 6.2 Nordics and Baltics

#### Denmark
- The open building data in Denmark carries no height. The INSPIRE Bygninger file (GeoDanmark footprints, 6,006,713 polygons, CC BY 4.0, no registration) has empty height, elevation and floor fields on every row; the BBR file (5,651,803 points, no registration) gives a floor count for every building but the text "other:unknown" for height. In the Copenhagen box the BBR floors agree with Overture's num_floors on 76.2 % of 4,111 pairs (95.6 % within one storey) and add storeys to 5,048 buildings where Overture has neither height nor floors, so BBR is the one open national source for a storey estimate. Both files are on Dataforsyningen as Wing FTP weblinks and need no login; the OGC API and the GeoDanmark WFS need a token.
- There is no national 3D model: "Danmark i 3D" is a 2019 benefit analysis and a 2024 follow-up, and as of 2025 SDFI was still looking for a mandate and funding (Kartverket's report, section 5.5). Dataforsyningen's catalogue (115 hits for the queries "bygning* OR 3D OR bygninger") lists no building 3D product, only DHM surface and terrain rasters from which heights can be derived; the data.europa.eu hub lists the DHM records (Surface, Terrain, Point Cloud, Origin) and a 2013 "Copenhagen 3D city model" record from datavejviser, and no state 3D-buildings record.
- Municipal models are open as CAD or SketchUp files only (Copenhagen 2013 DGN/DWG/DXF, Aarhus volume model for the area inside Ringvejen, Vejle, Horsens, Viborg). Vejle and Horsens say CC-BY-4.0; Copenhagen and Aarhus leave the licence field empty. None is a GIS format with attributes.
- Faroes: Føroyakort gives line maps, topographic maps and DSM/DTM; the DSM (2015) is derived from ArcticDEM. Greenland: Asiaq's technical base map covers towns and settlements; Dataforsyningen's GR1e service has a buildings layer (`asiaq_bygninger_1_20000`) for registered users.

#### Norway
- FKB-Bygning is the richest building dataset in the area (2.5D with roof edges and ridge lines, 1:1 to the Matrikkel building number, coverage 90 % of its extent per its metadata) but it is "Norge digitalt begrenset": download is limited to Norge digitalt agreement parties. Only the Matrikkelen Bygningspunkt (4,418,904 points with building type, number, status and SEFRAK ids, CC BY 4.0, anonymous WFS; no storey or height field in its schema) is open. A personal map built from open Norwegian data therefore has no footprints from the state.
- Kartverket's 2025 pre-project on volume geometry recommends treating volumes as a product derived from FKB and defining a CityGML specification; no public national dataset exists. Trondheim's 2014 model (36 km2, NLOD, 3ds Max project and VR file) is the only open city model found on Geonorge. Oslo, Bergen, Stavanger and Bodø are named as running their own models; no open download was found for them in Geonorge (queries "3D bygning", "bymodell", "CityGML", "Oslo 3D", "Bergen 3D", "Stavanger 3D") or on the data.europa.eu hub, where the Oslo, Bergen and Stavanger queries returned only orthophoto, point-cloud and unrelated records and Trondheim's model appears as copies of one record.
- Overture's Norwegian heights come from OSM and Microsoft ML (Oslo box: 26.3 % of buildings have a height, 85 % of those from Microsoft ML, 28 % have neither height nor floors).
- Svalbard: NPI's S100 map data is CC BY 4.0 at 1:100,000 detail; whether it holds a building layer was not read.

#### Sweden
- Lantmäteriet opened building footprints as a high-value dataset in February 2025 (CC BY 4.0, one GeoPackage per municipality through an open STAC catalogue; 290 files, about 1.3 GB), but the files sit behind a login (HTTP 401 on the file links) and the product page marks "Juridisk prövning: Ja" with usage terms for datasets containing personal data. It has purpose and name, no height or floors. The OSM import page records that Lantmäteriet's general permission to import into OSM was still pending.
- There is no national 3D building dataset: Kartverket's report calls Sweden "i en tidlig fase". Stockholm's own open data holds the first Swedish building heights in a real file: the LOD1 shapefiles (17.8 MB, 13 district files, 77,769 buildings, anonymous download) have BYGG_H = median roof height minus ground. In the central Stockholm box they add a height to 6,679 of 6,875 buildings, where Overture has one on 196 (the point match used each model's 3D centroid, so a few L-shaped buildings may match the wrong footprint). Stockholm's LOD2 set is order-only. Umeå's LOD1 (CC0, open exports; measured on one tile) is the one other Swedish municipal LOD1 with an open licence found. Göteborg, Malmö, Helsingborg and Uppsala: no open model found (the data.europa.eu hub query for Göteborg returned only Stockholm records; Helsingborg's SKP set is a page-only record). The 3CIM project (Stockholm, Göteborg, Malmö, Lund) produced a CityGML 2.0 information model and test data for one area per city; only secondary pages were read (see Unverified). Overture's Stockholm box is 80 % covered by num_floors but only 3.6 % by height, all from OSM.

#### Finland
- NLS's Buildings 3D is the national LoD2 product (CC BY 4.0, CityGML), produced from the 5 pts/m2 laser scanning and the 2D buildings, but "data is available only from a few example areas" and the product description's last update is 27 January 2022; Kartverket's report says LoD2 is not published openly for security reasons while LoD1 is open (that report's statement, not NLS's own page). The topographic database gives a storey class (1-2 or 3+ floors) for building polygons, open, updated yearly for buildings.
- Helsinki publishes a city-wide CityGML model (LoD1, LoD2, textured LoD2, CC BY 4.0, 4 km2 sheets, a WFS) and a 2017 reality mesh (OBJ, CC BY 4.0, 20 cm), but no city-wide file was reachable: the data.europa.eu record lists as downloads only the mesh, the Kalasatama CityGML (2019), the energy tables and a WFS at kartta.hel.fi/3d/citydb-wfs/wfs that answers 404, and a "3D-buildings of the City of Helsinki" LOD1 dataset (SKP and KML, CC BY 4.0) on avoindata.suomi.fi answers 403 (page only). The Energy and Climate Atlas building table (77,267 buildings, storeys on 46,917) is an opened file without geometry. The Kalasatama CityGML (2019) I read has a measuredHeight (highest roof minus ground, N2000) on 2,972 of 2,980 buildings and a storey count on 1,931. Espoo's model is a WFS under CityGML 2.0 (open data). Vantaa, Tampere, Turku and Oulu: nothing found (the hub shows only WFS, WMS and map-service records for Turku, Tampere and Oulu; Unverified). Åland: nothing found.
- avoindata.fi, hri.fi and avoindata.suomi.fi answered 403 from curl and WebFetch; I did not get round it. The HRI page was read through the Wayback copy.

#### Iceland
- Iceland has no open building footprint or height dataset that I could fetch: IS 50V Mannvirki holds points for structures outside towns plus town outlines at 1:50,000; Reykjavik's LUKR data (free, shapefile or DXF) needs an electronic-ID login, and the page itself says the 2009 massing model "provides only a rough picture". The data.europa.eu hub lists "Hús, Reykjavíkurborg" and "Staðfangaskrá" under the Icelandic geoportal and an ArcGIS Hub shapefile link for Reykjavik houses, but the Hub search API returned no items for hus, byggingar, building or 3D, so no file was reached (page only). Overture's Reykjavik box has heights on 0.3 % of buildings and neither height nor floors on 80.5 %.

#### Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania
- Estonia is the best case in the whole area: the Land Board's LOD2 file (856,360 buildings, free, CityGML/GDB/OBJ, a licence that allows commercial use and redistribution) is generated from laser scanning and ETAK roof outlines. Its `measuredHeight` is the highest roof point minus the lowest point of the building (checked on all 49,816 Tallinn buildings against z_max - z_min). In the Tallinn box Overture already has 4,950 heights on 6,556 buildings (75.5 %), 4,660 of them from the OSM record, and the OSM records of 3,970 of the 4,950 buildings with a height were last edited in 2025 (a block edit; no import page exists in OSM's Import/Catalogue); they agree with Maa-amet LoD2 to a median 0.58 m. Where the OSM edits did not reach (872 of 5,565 source buildings in the box), the Land Board file adds a height.
- Latvia: the State Land Service's cadastre footprints (INSPIRE Buildings, 1,450,651 polygons, CC BY 4.0) have no height or floors. Riga publishes an LOD1 model for the whole city (116,659 objects; BLDGHEIGHT is the maximum height; 2021 laser scanning; CC BY 4.0) and LOD2 for the historic centre and 13 areas (2022 scanning) plus the rest at LOD2 from 2021 scanning. Against Overture's Riga box, the LOD1 height is 1.7 m above the Microsoft/OSM heights on median and adds a height to 5,074 buildings.
- Lithuania: the national INSPIRE Buildings layer (2,162,959 polygons and 1,010,458 points, CC BY 4.0, WFS open) holds footprints only. No open national or city 3D building model was found (Vilnius and Kaunas models exist but their downloads or licences were not found: Unverified). The data.gov.lt open-data portal returned a bot-block page, which I did not get round.

### 6.3 Poland, Czechia, Slovakia and the south-east

**Poland.** GUGiK's 3D buildings are the one national, measured-height product of this area. The Polish hosts refuse connections from this Mac and from the mini (https and http), so the files were taken from Internet Archive captures (2025) of GUGiK's own downloads and opened: LoD2 of Warsaw (138,830 buildings, laser data 2012), Kraków (82,676, laser 2017) and radomski (90,677, laser 2011-2014), LoD1 of Warsaw (145,508, laser 2018, no `measuredHeight` in that capture), plus GUGiK's licence PDF and description PDF. The product page lists five layers: Buildings LoD2 (state of 2018; 236 powiaty in 10 voivodeships; CityGML 2.0 from ALS of 4 and 12 points/m2, BDOT10k BUBD_A footprints and a 1 m terrain model), and LoD1 for 2019, 2021, 2022 and 2024 (county packages for the whole country; flat roofs; roof = median of laser points in the footprint; ground = lowest terrain point in the footprint; the 2024 layers add `measuredHeight`). The rgugik package's county list (opened) gives LoD2 for 236 of 380 counties (all counties of voivodeships 04, 06, 12, 14, 16, 18, 20, 24, 26, 28): Warsaw, Kraków and Radom have it, Gdańsk, Łódź, Poznań and Wrocław only LoD1. The index is the identify tool on the geoportal layer (it returns the county's download links) and a WMS that carries the same links by GetFeatureInfo; live counts per county were not obtainable. Licence: public-sector information under the 2016 re-use Act, no fees; the user names the Główny Geodeta Kraju and www.geoportal.gov.pl, gives the retrieval date, states any processing and says the models are not a register; CC0 1.0 for what the Act does not regulate (attribution-only in effect; ODbL mixing not decided, so keep it a separate layer). Heights meant: LoD1 = median laser height; LoD2 = roof geometry, so the eave (lowest roof point) is about 5 m below the ridge in Warsaw (medians 12.0 and 17.2 m). Against Overture (section 10): the models add a height to 95 % of Warsaw-centre buildings, 76 % in Kraków's centre and nearly all of a Radom-county box; the LoD2 ridge height agrees with the OSM heights in Overture in Warsaw (ratio 1.02), while Overture's Microsoft heights in Kraków run 27 % below it (ratio 1.37). BDOT10k's BUBD_A holds `liczbaKondygnacji` (above-ground storeys; all residential buildings and non-residential of 40 m2 or more), and OSM's BDOT10k imports already put floors into Overture on 83.5 % of Warsaw-centre buildings (48 % in Kraków, 38.5 % in a Radom box), so BDOT10k storeys add little there and the heights are the gain; the BDOT10k fill rate itself is unmeasured and no BDOT10k file was read. Wrocław and Poznań show their own models in viewers with no file; Warsaw, Kraków, Gdańsk and Łódź: no city model download found (see Nothing better found and Unverified).

**Czechia.** There is no national 3D building model yet: ČÚZK's ZMVP (LoD2, from new laser scanning) is to be provided from the start of 2030. What exists now: RÚIAN (4.2 M building polygons with `pocetpodlazi` = above plus below ground storeys; CC BY 4.0) as the national storeys layer, ZABAGED (3,950,404 building polygons, no height or storeys; fields only name and kind), and the DSM DMP 1G (a surface model "including buildings and vegetation"). Prague has the best city model of the area (IPR: LoD2-equivalent plates for every building, 2001-2008 with a 2020 refresh, CityGML since 2023, CC BY; its scene layer `Budovy_3D_WSL1`, I3S mesh pyramids of 399,054,401 bytes, is open on the city's ArcGIS organization and its layer metadata was opened, attributes `ID_BUD`, `AKTUALIZAC`, `ML`, `ZMENA`, `KATUZE_KOD`, but no download link for the SHP, multipatch or CityGML sheets was found) and a separate storeys layer (178,775 polygons). Brno publishes RÚIAN buildings with measured heights (DSM minus terrain, 2020; 70,512 buildings; CC BY 4.0) and a LOD1 box model. Ostrava has LoD2.3 roof segments (eave and ridge as elevations above sea level) and a 2001 centre model; Plzeň a 2024 centre model in four map sheets.

**Slovakia.** The INSPIRE Buildings dataset from ZBGIS (CC BY 4.0, 3.6 M polygons, 93 % with `heightAboveGround` = top of construction minus lowest ground point, 99.7 % of those "measured") is the strongest finding of the area for heights. Floors are empty. Bratislava box: it adds a height to 83 % of the source buildings, and Overture's own heights there (mostly Microsoft) run lower (ratio 1.53). ÚGKK's ALS products (DTM 6.0, DSM 2.0, 0.5 m rasters, second cycle 2022-2026) are CC BY 4.0 and would give a second route. Bratislava's own "3D model budov v Bratislave v roku 2021" is a web application under CC-BY-NC-ND-4.0 (city Hub record), so unusable.

**Hungary.** Only a 2014 two-dimensional INSPIRE sample (Mesterszállás) was found; Lechner's 3D-based data infrastructure project (2015-2021) is described but no building height or LoD dataset was reachable. Budapest: nothing found.

**Romania.** ANCPI lists INSPIRE Buildings view and download services (2019-2020 records) but the host refuses connections; Bucharest and Cluj-Napoca: no open 3D or building-height dataset found.

**Bulgaria.** The cadastre's INSPIRE service gives every one of 3,284,195 buildings a floor count and nothing else (no heights). Sofiaplan's open catalogue (404 datasets) holds a 5 m DSM of Sofia (2017, "with buildings"), elevation maps and storey/density statistics per planning unit, but no per-building height layer; the cadastre service's metadata (opened) gives a use limitation naming "Tariff № 14 for the taxes, collected in the system of the ministry of regional development and public works" and "No limitations to public access" as access constraint; the tariff's text was not found.

**Moldova.** No building geometry on the portal: the CKAN API (`dataset.gov.md/api/3/action/package_search?q=cadastru`) returns 19 datasets of registers, statistics and land-cadastre tables (PDF, XLS, XLSX, CSV), none with geometry; the land-cadastre 2024 record states "License Not Specified". **Serbia.** RGZ's open data is the address register (points); no building layer.

### 6.4 Ukraine, Belarus, Russia, Caucasus, Turkey

**Ukraine.** The State GeoCadastre holds parcels, not buildings, and its map host did not connect. The national portal data.gov.ua (CKAN, reachable) has many municipal lists but no city-wide footprints with storeys: the only polygon set found is Pulyny's small aerial-survey GeoJSON (building.geojson 3.7 MB). Of the CKAN's 60 sets with GeoJSON and 6 with SHP, the only other building-related titles are a construction-activity register (Sheptytskyi) and Ivano-Frankivsk's urban-planning cadastre (two small GeoJSON layers, parcels and shelters, not buildings). Kyiv's open data is on data.gov.ua (97 datasets under the KMDA organization; the Address Register CSV is 21.3 MB, modified 2026-11-09, no storeys); its own site answers 403 and the GIS REST host does not connect. Lviv publishes a list of buildings with year and ownership (2018); its portal (Wayback, 2 Jul 2026) shows groups "Геодані" (6 sets) and "Будівництво" (6 sets), not opened, and the live host answers 403. Nothing with storeys per building found. Wartime restrictions on registers exist (a search answer, not fetched: not relied on). Damage-mapping sets (UNOSAT, Copernicus EMS) are assessments on footprints, not building models.

**Belarus.** nca.by (Wayback copy read) lists address and street registers and a technical-inventory function; no open building data; data.gov.by and the geoportal did not connect.

**Russia.** Nothing open with heights was reached. Moscow's address register (data.mos.ru dataset 60562, 3.1066 of 26 Sep 2023) has coordinates per object and no storeys (the moderator said so in 2016 and the promised multi-apartment-house set is not evidenced); data.mos.ru does not connect here, so it was read through Wayback. Saint Petersburg's city address database has coordinates (community report 2019). Reforma ZhKKh answers 403, but its open-data page (Wayback, 2022) documents a per-house export with floor_count_max, floor_count_min and built_year, generated on request after registration (a file is e-mailed in 30 minutes); no licence text on the archived page and no file opened. Moscow's "Объекты капитального строительства" (dataset 7703742961, 2016-2021) is a point set with address, use, built-up area and permit, with no storeys on the page. Rosreestr/NSPD did not connect, and its open-data list (Wayback, 11 Feb 2022) has 35 data sets, none a building set by title as far as read. The same hosts fail from the Mac mini as here (data.mos.ru, rosreestr.gov.ru, data.gov.ru, data.gov.by, nca.by, data.gov.ge, data.gov.am, classif.gov.spb.ru, cbs.csb.gov.tr), and kyivcity.gov.ua and the Lviv portal answer 403. 2GIS offers `floors` on building objects but its API rules forbid storing results; Yandex permits caching for 30 days only. OSM carries the storeys: 69 % of buildings in Moscow's box have num_floors, 62 % in Saint Petersburg, 56 % in Kazan, but heights on 1-2 %.

**Georgia, Armenia, Azerbaijan.** Georgia's NAPR runs the NSDI.GOV.GE geoportal (a JS shell: no data read) and published a draft open-data model on 23 Sep 2025; Tbilisi's maps portal and data.gov.ge gave nothing. Armenia's cadastre sites give menus only; the one Yerevan building layer found is an OSM extract. Azerbaijan's opendata.az lists 2,071 datasets; no building set found. Overture's Baku box is 75 % Microsoft ML footprints (OSM covers little there).

**Turkey.** The ministry's TUCBS Building theme specification (257 pages, v2.0 2022) defines above-ground storeys (`zeminUstuKatSayisi`), a measured height with reference levels and a 3D schema compatible with CityGML 3.0, but no dataset was found that carries it. The ministry stated in 2019 that 3D digital twins of the cities were complete and announced a further 3D city models programme in June 2025; no file, licence or tile service was found. Izmir is the one open municipal source: Bina Kimlik Belgesi CSVs (CC BY 4.0 portal licence) hold per-building identity certificates with storeys in SERBEST_KAT_ADEDI (filled on 24,687 of 24,688 Bayrakli rows and all 19,825 Bornova rows opened, most buildings 2 to 4; the server cut both downloads short), but only street address and parcel, no coordinates, so they cannot be matched to footprints without a geocoder. Izmir's CKAN lists 252 datasets, none a building geometry set. The ministry's cbs.csb.gov.tr (Wayback copy of 9 Jun 2026) lists a video "3 Boyutlu Bina ve Arazi Modeli" and a geographic-data permission page that names buildings among data needing permission; no download or terms for the 3D models were found. Istanbul's portal, Ankara's portal and TKGM's terms did not connect and have no Wayback snapshot.

## 8. Non-governmental sources

### 8.1 Germany

- ETHOS.BUILDA (Forschungszentrum Jülich): footprints with heights for all of Germany, 21.27 GB CSV in 17 files on Zenodo, ODbL-1.0, v7_20240429 (https://zenodo.org/api/records/11845992). Source licences stay with the contents; its 2022-23 sources.csv names which Länder LoD2 it used (BY, BE, BB, SN, ST, TH, HH, HE, NI, NW) and which fell back on OSM (BW, SH, SL, HB, MV, RP) (https://zenodo.org/records/11845992/files/sources.csv).
- Frantz et al., Building height map of Germany: 10 m raster of average building height within 50 m, Sentinel-based machine-learning regression trained on official 3D models, representative for about 2015, tiles 30x30 km, 532 MB zip, CC BY 4.0 (https://zenodo.org/api/records/4066295). Too coarse for single buildings.
- GlobalBuildingAtlas (TUM): the GBA.LoD1 repository states "distributed under the CC BY-NC 4.0 license" (non-commercial) and says its Part I polygons are ODbL on a separate repository; heights are on mediaTUM (https://huggingface.co/datasets/vitrobot/GBA.LoD1/raw/main/README.md). Not usable if a CC BY-NC layer cannot be mixed.
- Bavaria Building Dataset (TUM, mediaTUM 1709451): 18,205 tiles of 40 cm imagery with building masks, CC-BY (govdata resource licence); training data, not a height source (https://www.govdata.de/ckan/api/3/action/package_show?id=bavaria-building-dataset-bbd037ac).
- OSM and Esri inside Overture: heights in Overture come from Microsoft ML, "other (OSM record)" and, in Brandenburg, "Esri Community Maps" (CC BY 4.0 with OpenStreetMap waivers, version 2025-08-11) and a source labelled "USGS Lidar" (no licence field) (Overture sources read in the Lausitz box).
- Commercial (Cityweft, Google, HERE, TomTom, Cesium, 2GIS, Yandex): no terms read in this run.

### 8.2 Nordics and Baltics

- Cityweft (commercial): its Norway and Denmark pages say LoD1/LoD2 buildings are built from FKB and national DTM (Norway) and from lidar (Denmark). Only search snippets were seen; no terms were read. Not used.
- OpenStreetMap: the Norwegian "Norway Building Import" and Estonia's Maa-amet building geometry update (Harjumaa, in progress at its 2021-06-03 date; it replaces the old 2008 "Verbatium" outlines) are footprint imports; the import catalogue (Import/Catalogue, read in full) names no height import for Estonia, Finland, Denmark, Norway, Sweden, Latvia or Lithuania. Overture's Tallinn heights nevertheless show a block edit (the OSM records of 3,970 of the 4,950 buildings with a height have update_time in 2025) and agree with Maa-amet's heights (median abs diff 0.58 m): undocumented in the catalogue, but a government-derived set in practice.
- The Swedish OSM import page: Lantmäteriet footprints (9.3 million buildings) are awaiting a general permission to enter OSM, so they are not in Overture.

### 8.3 Poland, Czechia, Slovakia and the south-east

- **Overture itself** (release 2026-09-23.1): heights in the boxes measured here are rare: Prague 1.4 %, Brno 0.6 %, Sofia 0.2 %, Bratislava 11.9 % (nearly all Microsoft ML). Footprints are OSM nearly everywhere (Microsoft 2.7-7.5 % of the box). Floors: Prague 68 %, Brno 60 %, Bratislava 44 %, Sofia 30 %.
- **Cityweft** (commercial): "building-by-building models for all of Poland", LOD2 with heights, roofs and footprints, built on "National authoritative datasets" and 1 m topography, 10+ export formats, pricing page; terms not read. Source: https://cityweft.com/coverage/poland
- **OSM**: Warsaw 3D hand-modelling table (2013) at https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/wiki/Warszawa/Warszawa_w_3D (a log of modelled landmarks; not a bulk import). No OSM height import from GUGiK, ČÚZK or ÚGKK was looked up.
- Not covered: 2GIS, Yandex, HERE, TomTom, Google, Cesium coverage of this area (terms not read).
- **OpenStreetMap imports of BDOT10k (Poland)**: the OSM wiki pages "BDOT10k buildings import" (automated, account NorthCrab_upload, 2023; tags `building:levels` and `source:building=BDOT`; ODbL compliance recorded as verified; data source cited as geoportal.gov.pl) and "Import/Poland Buildings" (Esri/RapiD set of 375,281 buildings prepared Jan 2023; "explicit permission" for OSM cited) were read; they explain why Overture already has floors on 83.5 % of Warsaw-centre buildings, and mean GUGiK's BDOT10k storeys are partly in Overture already. Sources: https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/w/index.php?title=BDOT10k_buildings_import&action=raw and https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/w/index.php?title=Import/Poland_Buildings&action=raw

### 8.4 Ukraine, Belarus, Russia, Caucasus, Turkey

- **OSM** carries the storeys in the Russian-speaking cities (Overture boxes: Moscow 69 %, Kyiv 63 %, Minsk 55 % of buildings with num_floors) and little in Turkey (Istanbul 1.8 %, Izmir 0.2 %). OSM heights are 0.2-3 %. All Overture footprints here are OSM or Microsoft ML; no other supplier appears.
- **2GIS** Places API: field `items.floors`; rules clause 4.2 forbids storing the data beyond caching geocoding results (https://law.2gis.ru/api-rules). Not usable for an offline map.
- **Yandex** JS API: caching up to 30 days only, no storage (https://yandex.com/dev/tariffs/doc/en/jsapi/terms/).
- **Cityweft / Texty.org.ua 3D models**: Cityweft's global buildings page is a Notion page that did not render; Texty's 3D models of Kyiv were built from OSM (their article). Nothing beyond OSM.
- Yerevan's university ArcGIS layer is a Geofabrik extract of OSM (fields osm_id, fclass): ODbL.

## 9. The Overture sample

Method as Europe-W section 19: S3 listing https://overturemaps-us-west-2.s3.amazonaws.com/?list-type=2&prefix=release/2026-09-23.1/theme%3Dbuildings/type%3Dbuilding/ (512 parts, 2,533,842,612 rows) read with pyarrow's anonymous S3 filesystem; footers of all parts saved as e1326/shared/overture-index.json; per box only the row groups whose bbox statistics overlap were read (columns id, bbox, height, num_floors, sources).

Reading the table. (1) Dataset names that supply footprints or heights in the 28 boxes: OpenStreetMap (footprints in all boxes, 78-99 % of buildings in all but Riga (72 %) and Tbilisi (63 %)), Microsoft ML Buildings (a minority of footprints everywhere, 3-37 %; Tbilisi 10,665 of 28,984, Ankara 2,478), and Esri Community Maps (Riga 1,650 footprints, Helsinki 21; no height). Nothing else supplies a footprint or a height in these boxes: no national dataset appears, with no Estonian, Danish or Czech source in the list. Where a height has no `height` source entry, it is the OSM record's own value ("other (OSM record)"). (2) Microsoft ML supplies most heights in Western and Central European boxes (Oslo 85 %, Copenhagen 81 %, Riga 87 %, Vilnius 91 %, Krakow 75 %, Bratislava 79 %, Munich 71 %, rural Germany 79 %). OSM's own height tag dominates where it is a community or import effort: Tallinn (4,660 of 4,950 heights, 75.5 % of buildings: by far the best-covered capital, see the Estonia notes), Berlin 55 %, Hamburg 50 %. In Eastern Europe, Turkey and the Caucasus Overture has almost no heights at all (Sofia 0.2 %, Tbilisi 0.2 %, Istanbul 0.6 %, Kyiv 0.8 %, St Petersburg 0.8 %, Prague 1.3 %, Bucharest 1.6 %, Budapest 1.7 %, Moscow 1.8 %), all of them OSM height tags, and Microsoft ML supplies none there. (3) num_floors is high where OSM has building:levels (Warsaw 83 %, Stockholm 80 %, Prague 70 %, Moscow 77 %, Berlin 74 %, Kyiv 63 %, St Petersburg 60 %), and low in Istanbul (2.1 %), Ankara (6.4 %), Tbilisi (16 %), Bucharest (17 %). (4) The real gap is buildings with neither: Istanbul 97.5 %, Ankara 93.5 %, Tbilisi 83.6 %, Bucharest 82.4 %, Reykjavik 80.5 %, Sofia 72.2 %, Copenhagen 62.4 %, rural Germany 55.7 %; the best are Warsaw 15.7 %, Stockholm 19.0 %, Tallinn 19.8 %. (5) Rural boxes: rural Germany has 42.6 % of buildings with a height (Microsoft ML covers Germany) and 3.2 % with floors; the Polish rural box has no height at all (Microsoft ML supplies no height in the Warsaw and Radom boxes, though it does in Krakow) and 52 % floors, so Poland's heights are rare outside a few cities (Krakow 21.3 %, from Microsoft ML). (6) Caveats: boxes are single neighbourhoods (0.05 degree urban, 0.1 rural, buildings counted when the bbox min corner lies in the box); the part number is the file of release 2026-09-23.1 (`part-NNNNN` under .../theme=buildings/type=building/); row groups were selected by their bbox statistics in overture-index.json (all 512 part footers, 11 MB); bytes read per box a few MB. Heights counted from the `height` column, floors from `num_floors`; "supplier" is the sources entry whose property contains "height".

Reading: Overture is nearly height-less where NRW, Bayern and Berlin LoD2 exist (4.8 %, 15.6 %, 17.9 % of buildings), so those LoD2 add a height to 80-96 % of the source buildings. Brandenburg is the opposite: Overture already holds heights close to the state's. Where both exist, ridge-based LoD2 heights are 1.2 (Berlin, NRW) to 1.5 (Bayern) times Overture's (Microsoft ML and OSM). ### 10.2 Nordics and Baltics

Method as Europe-W section 20, with the shared tool e1326/shared/overlap_tool.py (`overlap`): one representative point per source building (point on the footprint, or for Riga a point inside the building's lowest triangle, for the BBR points the point itself) -> the smallest Overture footprint of release 2026-09-23.1 that contains it (duckdb spatial; Overture read from S3 by row-group statistics). "Adds a height" = the source has a height and the matched Overture building has none, or nothing matches. Height meanings: Estonia = highest roof point minus lowest point of the building (measuredHeight, checked equal to z_max - z_min); Riga LOD1 = the building's maximum height (BLDGHEIGHT) above its base elevation; Helsinki Kalasatama = measuredHeight, equal to highest roof minus ground level in the file (a ridge-type value, N2000 based); Denmark = none.

| Source and box | Source buildings | Matched to an Overture footprint | Matched with an Overture height (by supplier) | Source buildings that add a height Overture lacks | Height comparison where both | Bytes read (source) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Maa-amet LOD2 buildings, Tallinn file, box 24.725..24.775 x 59.415..59.465 (Overture box: 6,556 buildings, 4,950 with a height = 75.5 %) | 5,565 with a footprint point in the box (of 49,816 in the Tallinn file; 137 of those have no ground polygon) | 5,403 (97.1 %); 162 unmatched | 4,693 = 86.9 % of matched: other (OSM record) 4,358, Microsoft ML 335 | 872 = 15.7 % of source (heights); no storeys in the product | 4,693 pairs: median abs diff 0.58 m, signed (source minus Overture) +0.28 m, median ratio 1.03, within 20 % 81.0 % | 33.0 MB CityGML zip (568 MB GML unpacked, deleted); 36.5 MB gdb zip read for the fields (deleted) |
| Riga LOD1 (CityGML), box 24.08..24.13 x 56.925..56.975 (Overture box: 7,327 buildings, 1,855 with a height = 25.3 %) | 6,723 building objects with a base point in the box (of 116,659 in the file) | 5,061 (75.3 %); 1,662 unmatched | 1,649 = 32.6 % of matched: Microsoft ML 1,594, other (OSM record) 55 | 5,074 = 75.5 % of source | 1,649 pairs: median abs diff 2.51 m, signed +1.72 m, median ratio 1.25, within 20 % 28.0 % (the LOD1 maximum height is higher than the Microsoft/OSM heights) | 178 MB CityGML zip (2.4 GB GML, deleted) |

Reading it. Estonia's LOD2 adds a height to 15.7 % of the Tallinn buildings and agrees with Overture's OSM heights (median 0.58 m, 81 % within 20 %): Overture's Tallinn heights are already close to the Land Board's, so the gain there is the 872 buildings and every other town where the OSM edit has not reached (Overture's Tartu and rural Estonia were not sampled). Riga's LOD1 is nearly pure gain (Overture has a height on 25 % of buildings, source 75 % adds one), but its heights run 1.7 m above Overture's: the publisher's "maximum height" is a ridge-like value, so an offset or a roof-height-to-eave conversion is needed before mixing (as for NRCan in the Americas report). Helsinki's Kalasatama sample adds heights to 65 % of its buildings and storeys to 356; the model's whole-city files were not reachable from here (the HRI page returned 403 and the WFS 404), so this is a sample from an area that the 2019 file covers. Denmark's open files add no height; BBR adds a storey count to 35 % of the points in Copenhagen, and where it overlaps Overture's num_floors it agrees on 76 % (95.6 % within a storey). Caveats: boxes are single neighbourhoods; point matching on large buildings and on multi-part buildings (Denmark) can overcount matches; Copenhagen's BBR "matched with a height" counts several BBR points per Overture building; Riga's LOD1 base points use the lowest triangle; Tallinn's 137 buildings without a ground polygon were dropped.

### 10.3 Poland, Czechia, Slovakia and the south-east

Method (as in Europe-W section 20): source building -> one representative point (ST_PointOnSurface) -> smallest Overture footprint containing it; Overture release 2026-09-23.1 read with the shared `overlap_tool.py` and `overture-index.json` (only row groups whose bbox statistics overlap the box; a 0.05-degree box each). "Adds a height" = source has a height and the matched Overture building has none or none matches; for storeys-only sources the "adds" column counts source buildings with storeys where Overture has neither height nor floors. Overture's supplier of a height is the `/properties/height` source, "other (OSM record)" where the entry is absent. Heights meant: Brno `vyska_zmer` = surface model minus terrain model inside the building (measured); Slovakia = highest point of construction minus lowest ground point (top-of-roof, ridge-level); storeys: Prague = total storeys of main volumes, RÚIAN = above plus below ground, Bulgaria = above ground only. GUGiK's LoD2 heights are computed from the roof geometry: ridge = highest roof vertex minus mean ground, eave = lowest roof vertex minus ground, mean roof = mean of roof vertices minus ground (the Warsaw LoD1 is the median laser height); the files were read from Internet Archive captures because the Polish hosts refuse connections (see Unverified).

### 10.4 Ukraine, Belarus, Russia, Caucasus, Turkey

No overlap with polygons could be run for these countries: every source that would hold building polygons with storeys or heights was unreachable or does not hold them (data.mos.ru, data.ibb.gov.tr, the Kyiv GIS server and Rosreestr did not connect; Moscow's address register is a point list without storeys or heights; Izmir's Bina Kimlik Belgesi has no coordinates). The method that would apply is Europe-W section 20 (centroid in footprint, release 2026-09-23.1). The one reachable per-building table is Izmir's:

| Source and box | Source buildings | Matched to an Overture footprint | Matched with an Overture height (by supplier) | Source buildings that add a height or storeys Overture lacks | Height comparison where both | Bytes read |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Izmir Bina Kimlik Belgesi, Bayrakli and Bornova (Izmir box 27.12..27.17 x 38.40..38.45 holds 52,769 Overture buildings) | Bayrakli 24,688 rows read (23,815 distinct building ids) and Bornova 19,825 (downloads cut short by the server); no coordinates (address and block/parcel only) | not measurable: no geometry or point to put in a footprint | not measurable | storeys (SERBEST_KAT_ADEDI) on 24,687 of 24,688 Bayrakli rows and all 19,825 Bornova rows; Overture's Izmir box has floors on 128 buildings (0.2 %), so a geocoded join would add storeys to nearly all | none: the source has no height, only storey counts (meaning inferred as above-ground storeys) | about 13.6 MB and 4.3 MB of CSV (deleted) |

What the source's value means: floor-count fields of an earthquake building identity certificate (above-ground normal floors, basement floors, total); not a roof or eave height.

Reading it: Overture holds floors (OSM `building:levels`) on 55-69 % of buildings in the Russian, Ukrainian and Belarusian boxes and 15-28 % in Tbilisi, Yerevan and Baku, but only 0.2-1.8 % in the three Turkish boxes (Istanbul 1.8 %, Ankara 6.9 %, Izmir 0.2 %); heights are 0.2-3 % everywhere. Baku's box is 75 % Microsoft ML footprints. So Turkish cities have the largest gap of storeys, and a Turkish national source would matter most, but none is open.

| Hungary: data.gov.hu, Budapest 3D, Lechner national building or height product, cadastre (földhivatal) | data.gov.hu does not connect; EU portal harvest; Lechner pages; web search | Nothing open found; not proof of absence |
| Romania: ANCPI INSPIRE BU services, Bucharest and Cluj 3D | geoportal.ancpi.ro and data.gov.ro do not connect; EU portal records; web search | Attributes and coverage of ANCPI BU unknown |
| Bulgaria: text of "Tariff № 14", KAIS terms, Sofia 3D | the INSPIRE metadata record (opened), inspire.cadastre.bg service pages, kais.cadastre.bg and www.cadastre.bg front pages (read, no terms), data.egov.bg (403) | The use limitation names the tariff; its text and whether it prices this service were not found |
| Moldova: geoportal and cadastre (ASRC) | geoportal.md refuses, cadastru.md answers 403, dataset.gov.md answers | Not reached |
| Serbia: RGZ Basic Topographic Model, Belgrade 3D, Geosrbija | preview.rgz.gov.rs (page text empty), geosrbija.rs and geoportal.rgz.gov.rs refuse | Whether a building layer is sold or open unknown |

## 12. Accidental

### 12.1 Germany

- Overture's heights in Brandenburg are already the state's: Potsdam tiles match with median ratio 0.99 and 82 % within 20 %, rural Lausitz 0.98 and 67 %; suppliers "Esri Community Maps" 5,562 of 7,242 height records in the 0.05° Potsdam box and "USGS Lidar" 250 (a label that does not fit German data). Inference: the Esri Community Maps contribution is built from the LGB open data.
- Rostock (69.0 % of buildings with a height, 7,589 of 9,013 from OSM records) and Magdeburg (55.4 %) are the other height-rich German boxes; Düsseldorf is the poorest (4.1 %).
- NRW's OGC API (ldproxy) serves CityJSON 1.1 (`items?f=cityjson&bbox=...`), glTF and 3D Tiles; the items query without `f=cityjson` returned HTTP 400. Its collection licence is https://www.govdata.de/dl-de/zero-2-0.
- Niedersachsen's bucket https://lod2.opengeodata.lgln.niedersachsen.de/ lists as S3 (ListBucket XML, 1,000 keys per page): 37,940 tiles each as .gml, .zip and a 530-byte .json.
- NRW's tile listing has 2,376 files under 2 kB (empty tiles) among 35,022.
- Sachsen-Anhalt's INSPIRE 2D WFS ignored STARTINDEX (four requests returned the same 250 features).
- BKG: "ab ca. 2027 wird nur noch das Format Geopackage angeboten" for HU-DE.
- NRW "Grundrissdaten" NAS for one city (Bottrop) unpacks to 8 GB from a 106 MB zip; deleted.
- The Berlin tile has 763 buildings, 962 BuildingParts and 5 buildings without a GroundSurface.

### 12.2 Nordics and Baltics

- Dataforsyningen's website runs an open Elasticsearch behind the catalogue (`POST https://dataforsyningen.dk/es/products/_search` with an `ids` query returns a product's tags, summary and links); it is how I read the product records without the JavaScript app.
- Geonorge's catalogue has an open JSON API (`kartkatalog.geonorge.no/api/search?text=...` and `/api/getdata/<uuid>`) with licence, constraints and quality figures (FKB-Bygning "Prosentvis dekning ... 90").
- Lantmäteriet's STAC catalogue (`api.lantmateriet.se/stac-vektor/v1`) is open for browsing while its data files answer 401: the building files total about 1.3 GB for 290 municipalities.
- Danish files without registration: both INSPIRE building files (Wing FTP weblinks found in the Dataforsyningen product records) download anonymously at about 3 MB/s.
- Estonia's CityGML carries `measuredHeight` that is exactly z_max - z_min of the model; Riga's LOD1 is a `GenericCityObject` (not `bldg:Building`) with BLDGHEIGHT and BASEELEV as generic attributes and an easting/northing swap in its coordinate order (first value northing).
- Helsinki's Kalasatama CityGML (2019) is the only CityGML left in 3d.hel.ninja/data/citygml/; the city-wide model sits behind a HRI page that returns 403 to non-browser clients.
- Kartverket's 2025 pre-project report (68 pages) is a compact comparison of the national 3D programmes of Estonia, Finland, Sweden, Denmark, the Netherlands, Switzerland, Poland and France; section 5 is worth reading for the other regions' reports.
- Overture 2026-09-23.1 lists Esri Community Maps as a footprint supplier in Riga (1,650 buildings) and Helsinki; no height comes from it.
- The shared index and tool: e1326/shared/overture-index.json (512 parts, 2,533,842,612 rows, footers read in 40 s) and e1326/shared/overlap_tool.py (functions rows_for_bbox, load_overture, sample, overlap with a `source_sql` mode for CityGML or GDB sources converted to Parquet first).
- data.europa.eu's hub search API (`https://data.europa.eu/api/hub/search/search?q=...&filters=dataset&limit=N`, and `/search/datasets/<id>?useNormalizedId=true&locale=en`) answers anonymously and carries titles, descriptions, distributions with licences from the national catalogues (nsip-se, oppnadata, open-data-finland and others); a `catalogs` filter is not accepted as a plain query parameter. - Stockholm's zip has Latin-1 folder names ("Södermalm" and others) that macOS `unzip` refuses to create; Python's zipfile unpacks it with a sanitized name.

### 12.3 Poland, Czechia, Slovakia and the south-east

- The EU data portal's hub API (`https://data.europa.eu/api/hub/search/search?q=...&filter=dataset&facets={"country":["pl"]}` and `/datasets/<id>`) carries the Polish, Czech, Slovak, Bulgarian, Romanian and Moldovan portals' records, with distribution links; it answered when the Polish portal's own API did not. Descriptions are often machine translated (the Polish 3D-building record's English text is a translation).
- Prague's open-data hub is `opendata.geoportalpraha.cz` and its only collection is `all` (`/api/search/v1/collections/all/items?q=...`; `collections/dataset` returns 404). The same call on `data.brno.cz` works.
- The Czech RÚIAN viewing service answers `f=geojson` with `outSR=4326` and pages of 2,000; its storeys field counts below-ground levels too (the Brno box agrees with Overture floors to within a storey on 88.8 %).
- The Bulgarian cadastre service returns GeoJSON pages that end early (997 of 1,000) while `returnCountOnly` says 24,821; paging by offset loses most rows. Fetching ids (`returnIdsOnly`) and POSTing `objectIds` in chunks of 500 gives all of them.
- The Slovak INSPIRE geodatabase has two layers (`BuildingP` 25,609 points, `BuildingS` 3.6 M polygons; the default layer is the points), `heightAboveGround` holds a nodata value (-32,737), and the zip carries its own licence PDF ("CC-BY 4.0"). Downloading 418 MB from opendata.skgeodesy.sk took about 15 minutes; it resumes (HTTP 206).
- Brno's "Výška budov" shows what a cheap national method yields: the Czechitas 2020 layer is DMP 1G minus DMR 5G per RÚIAN building plus estimates from the number of flats and floors, openly licensed.
- The Internet Archive's CDX index (`web.archive.org/cdx/search/cdx?url=<host>/<path>/*&collapse=urlkey&fl=original,timestamp,statuscode,length`) lists archived downloads with their sizes; a capture is fetched as `web.archive.org/web/<timestamp>id_/<original url>`. It flips between answering and a "Temporarily Offline" 503 page (an HTML body), so retry and check that the body does not start with `<`; curl needs `-g` for URLs containing `[` or `*`. GUGiK's `pobierz.php?d=2&plik=powiaty/1465_gml.zip` style download links were archived for only Warsaw, Kraków and one other county (0663). - The R package `rgugik` (GitHub, kadyb/rgugik) documents GUGiK's download URL patterns and carries the county list with a LOD2 flag as package data (`data/county_names.rda`, read with pyreadr): an offline way to know which powiaty have LoD2 (236 of 380). - GUGiK's LoD2 files are 1:10,000 sheet tiles whose coordinates are EPSG:2180 (x easting, y northing in `gml:pos`); a county ZIP unzips to about 20-25 times its size (Warsaw 112 MB to 2.5 GB). Heights are above the Kronsztadt 86 datum, so a building's height must be computed from ground and roof vertices (no height attribute). - In Warsaw's LoD2 the building's lowest roof vertex (eave) is 4.5 to 6 m lower than its highest (ridge) at the median, so the height definition changes comparisons with OSM heights by 20 to 30 %: the ridge matches OSM (ratio 1.02), the eave does not (0.80). - Prague's open Hub item "Budovy 3D / Buildings 3D model" is a web scene that points to two I3S scene layers on `services5.arcgis.com/SBTXIEUGWbqzUecw`; the scene layer API (`.../SceneServer/layers/0?f=json`) answers without a login and gives the attribute names and extent.

### 12.4 Ukraine, Belarus, Russia, Caucasus, Turkey

- data.gov.ua's CKAN `package_search` fails (empty result) when a `fl` parameter is passed, and returns nothing for unstemmed Ukrainian words ("поверховість" 0 hits, "кількість поверхів" 3); the `fq=organization:` form lists an organization's datasets.
- Izmir's CKAN lists a CSV of 27 MB on its record but the file served 13.6 MB (the record's size may be a different encoding); the Bornova file download was cut short at about 3.8 MB in the time allowed.
- maps.mail.ru hosts a working Overpass endpoint when overpass-api.de does not connect (rate limits apply).
- Wayback copies of Russian pages work for data.mos.ru, nca.by and rosreestr.gov.ru where the live hosts do not connect.
